correct in both environments the race created. **★ THE TEST CORRECTED ME TWICE:** v1 asserted a naive column drifts by the process offset → **FAILED**, because a JS round-trip is **symmetric and Rewritten, Case 3 spawns a child under **`TZ=America/New_York`** and asks **Postgres**: a token with an hour left is stored naive as `13:27` and judged **ALREADY EXPIRED, 4h early** (lockout west of UTC; **an extended password-reset window east of it**) — timestamptz judges it correctly. Investigation found the capability was **already built and never wired to a question**: `planner:explain` was registered, bound, permission-checked and ownership-walked, but **no resolver matcher pointed at it** — every `explain` matcher belonged to `nutrition-knowledge` or the opportunity card. So why-questions fell through to `planner.read`, which returns the plan's **contents**, and the LLM — under a prompt telling it to "SYNTHESISE… connect the facts" and "EXPLAIN THE 'WHY'" — wrote the rationale itself. **Sharpest finding: the handler's "honest gap" was asserting a falsehood.** For any ordinarily-planned meal it said *"the Planner records no selection rationale for this meal… will not fabricate one"* — but the Planner **had** a full 14-dimension cited evidence trail for it, on its own screen; only the wiring was missing. A gap that reports the platform's own plumbing as an absence of knowledge is a confident false statement, and worse than silence because it forecloses the question. **Constraint that shaped the design:** planner explanations are **never persisted** (no suggest-session table across 95 tables; `planner_entries` has no explanation column), and schema changes were forbidden — so the Companion **recomputes from the same owner**, exactly as the opportunity handler already does. Proven **pre-existing** by reconstructing the pre-COMP4 tree from the snapshot (`git archive` + `git apply` + untar, verified free of COMP4 code) and reproducing the identical failures; the 57 hidden suites were then **run directly and all pass**.
